class=\"timeline-item__card-img\"><img loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\" data-src=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/mercy-Image-1988.jpg\" https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/mercy-Image-1988-300x192.jpg 300w\" sizes=\"(max-width: 498px) 100vw, 498px\"><\/noscript><\/div>\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">1988<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">MERCY HOUSE IS INCORPORATED<\/span><p>Father Jerome T. Karcher first began reflecting seriously on the plight of those struggling with homelessness in 1987 while he was Associate Pastor at Saint Norbert\u2019s Church in Orange, California. His first efforts were to organize a small group of friends to assist the homeless. As Associate Pastor at Saint Anne Catholic Church in Santa Ana, he became more aware of the needs of both homeless and immigrant populations. One experience concerning the needs of an immigrant young man who was living on the streets in an abandoned garage particularly moved him to respond to the local situation of homelessness. This decision inspired him to found Mercy House as a non-profit charitable corporation.<\/p><\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">Our Founder, Father Jerome Karcher <\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">1988<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">MERCY HOUSE IS INCORPORATED<\/span><p>Father Jerome T. Karcher first began reflecting seriously on the plight of those struggling with homelessness in 1987 while he was Associate Pastor at Saint Norbert\u2019s Church in Orange, California. His first efforts were to organize a small group of friends to assist the homeless. As Associate Pastor at Saint Anne Catholic Church in Santa Ana, he became more aware of the needs of both homeless and immigrant populations. One experience concerning the needs of an immigrant young man who was living on the streets in an abandoned garage particularly moved him to respond to the local situation of homelessness. This decision inspired him to found Mercy House as a non-profit charitable corporation.<\/p><\/div><\/div><\/div><div class=\"jet-timeline-item  elementor-repeater-item-2d7a8fe jet-timeline-item--image-inside\">\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card\">\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-inner\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-img\"><img loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\" data-src=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/609e92d273c1f50eb82b9255_History-1989-Nov.jpg\" data-srcset=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/609e92d273c1f50eb82b9255_History-1989-Nov.jpg 640w, https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/609e92d273c1f50eb82b9255_History-1989-Nov-300x300.jpg 300w, https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/609e92d273c1f50eb82b9255_History-1989-Nov-150x150.jpg 150w\"   src=\"data:image\/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP\/\/\/yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7\" class=\"attachment-full size-full wp-image-1307 lazyload\" alt=\"History - 1989 Nov\"  sizes=\"(max-width: 640px) 100vw, 640px\"  \/><noscript><img loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\" width=\"640\" height=\"640\" src=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/609e92d273c1f50eb82b9255_History-1989-Nov.jpg\" class=\"attachment-full size-full wp-image-1307\" alt=\"History - 1989 Nov\" srcset=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/609e92d273c1f50eb82b9255_History-1989-Nov.jpg 640w, https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/609e92d273c1f50eb82b9255_History-1989-Nov-300x300.jpg 300w, https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/02\/609e92d273c1f50eb82b9255_History-1989-Nov-150x150.jpg 150w\" sizes=\"(max-width: 640px) 100vw, 640px\"><\/noscript><\/div>\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">November 1989<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House is first Introduced to the Community<\/span><p>An article in the Diocese of Orange \"Bulletin\" in November 1989 first announced Father Jerome\u2019s work with the homeless. This article caught the attention of Patrick Powers and the Warren P. Powers Foundation who made the first significant contribution that enabled Mercy House to begin services and hire its first employees.<\/p> <\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">A scan of the article that first introduced Mercy House <\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">November 1989<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House is first Introduced to the Community<\/span><p>An article in the Diocese of Orange \"Bulletin\" in November 1989 first announced Father Jerome\u2019s work with the homeless. This article caught the attention of Patrick Powers and the Warren P. Powers Foundation who made the first significant contribution that enabled Mercy House to begin services and hire its first employees.<\/p> <\/div><\/div><\/div><div class=\"jet-timeline-item  elementor-repeater-item-88b6d3b jet-timeline-item--image-inside\">\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card\">\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-inner\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">Established January 1990<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House Begins Services<\/span><p>Father Jerome\u2019s first project was to create a transitional residence for employable homeless adult men which opened in 1990 in a rented a house on North Main Street in Santa Ana. It provided shelter for 10 men and was simply called Mercy House. The \u201coriginal\u201d Mercy House eventually became known as Joseph Residence. It moved to a permanent location and is now a large, 4-unit apartment complex that houses 21 formerly homeless single men including veterans. Joseph Residence was remodeled through partnership with HomeAid Orange County.<\/p><\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">Father Jerome helping build what is now called Joseph Residence.<\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">Established January 1990<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House Begins Services<\/span><p>Father Jerome\u2019s first project was to create a transitional residence for employable homeless adult men which opened in 1990 in a rented a house on North Main Street in Santa Ana. It provided shelter for 10 men and was simply called Mercy House. The \u201coriginal\u201d Mercy House eventually became known as Joseph Residence. It moved to a permanent location and is now a large, 4-unit apartment complex that houses 21 formerly homeless single men including veterans. Joseph Residence was remodeled through partnership with HomeAid Orange County.<\/p><\/div><\/div><\/div><div class=\"jet-timeline-item  elementor-repeater-item-a6b1788 jet-timeline-item--image-inside\">\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card\">\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-inner\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-img\"><img loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\" data-src=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1990-May-2.jpg\" data-srcset=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1990-May-2.jpg 600w, https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1990-May-2-300x300.jpg 300w, https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1990-May-2-150x150.jpg 150w\"   src=\"data:image\/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP\/\/\/yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7\" class=\"attachment-full size-full wp-image-3475 lazyload\" alt=\"1990 May-2\"  sizes=\"(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px\"  \/><noscript><img loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\" width=\"600\" height=\"600\" src=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1990-May-2.jpg\" class=\"attachment-full size-full wp-image-3475\" alt=\"1990 May-2\" srcset=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1990-May-2.jpg 600w, https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1990-May-2-300x300.jpg 300w, https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1990-May-2-150x150.jpg 150w\" sizes=\"(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px\"><\/noscript><\/div>\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">1990<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">First staff members are hired<\/span><p>It was clear to Father Jerome that God\u2019s Providence was purposeful as Mercy House received substantial contributions to hire two important staff members in 1990 who would lay the foundation for its services to the poor, the marginalized and those struggling with homelessness. The first was Larry Haynes who is now the Chief Executive Officer for Mercy House and has creatively overseen its expansion of programs and services. The second was Lisa Marcus whose insights and commitment as the Development Director served this growing organization for 15 years.<\/p><\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">Now CEO, Larry Haynes, in his early years at Mercy House<\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">1990<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">First staff members are hired<\/span><p>It was clear to Father Jerome that God\u2019s Providence was purposeful as Mercy House received substantial contributions to hire two important staff members in 1990 who would lay the foundation for its services to the poor, the marginalized and those struggling with homelessness. The first was Larry Haynes who is now the Chief Executive Officer for Mercy House and has creatively overseen its expansion of programs and services. The second was Lisa Marcus whose insights and commitment as the Development Director served this growing organization for 15 years.<\/p><\/div><\/div><\/div><div class=\"jet-timeline-item  elementor-repeater-item-3dc54a2 jet-timeline-item--image-inside\">\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card\">\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-inner\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">April 1994<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Regina Residence Opens<\/span><p>In April 1994, the Mercy House vision expanded further to embrace the needs of homeless mothers and their children by opening Regina House, now called Regina Residence. Seven families may maintain residency there for up to one year while the mothers are employed, pursuing higher education or enrolled in a job-training program. Regina Residence was constructed through the generosity and assistance of HomeAid Orange County and Fieldstone and was furnished by Munro\u2019s Furniture.<\/p><\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">Regina Residence when it first opened in 1994<\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">April 1994<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Regina Residence Opens<\/span><p>In April 1994, the Mercy House vision expanded further to embrace the needs of homeless mothers and their children by opening Regina House, now called Regina Residence. Seven families may maintain residency there for up to one year while the mothers are employed, pursuing higher education or enrolled in a job-training program. Regina Residence was constructed through the generosity and assistance of HomeAid Orange County and Fieldstone and was furnished by Munro\u2019s Furniture.<\/p><\/div><\/div><\/div><div class=\"jet-timeline-item  elementor-repeater-item-d17eac9 jet-timeline-item--image-inside\">\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card\">\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-inner\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">July 1999<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House Center Opens in Santa Ana<\/span><p>As the vision of Mercy House grew, it began to consider a continuum of services for the marginalized and homeless. This expanding perspective created the Mercy House Center which opened in 1999 as a walk-in outreach program designed to address the immediate needs of Orange County\u2019s homeless population through crisis intervention, food and shelter referral, clothing vouchers, transportation assistance and hygiene items. First located at First United Methodist Church in Santa Ana then later at St. Joseph Church in Santa Ana. The program operated for 11 years until it closed in 2010.<\/p><\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">The first employees of the Mercy House Center in 1999<\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">July 1999<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House Center Opens in Santa Ana<\/span><p>As the vision of Mercy House grew, it began to consider a continuum of services for the marginalized and homeless. This expanding perspective created the Mercy House Center which opened in 1999 as a walk-in outreach program designed to address the immediate needs of Orange County\u2019s homeless population through crisis intervention, food and shelter referral, clothing vouchers, transportation assistance and hygiene items. First located at First United Methodist Church in Santa Ana then later at St. Joseph Church in Santa Ana. The program operated for 11 years until it closed in 2010.<\/p><\/div><\/div><\/div><div class=\"jet-timeline-item  elementor-repeater-item-ed1c92d jet-timeline-item--image-inside\">\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card\">\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-inner\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-img\"><img loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\" data-src=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1999-Prevention.jpg\" data-srcset=\"https:\/\/mercyhouse.net\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/03\/1999-Prevention.jpg 1920w, class=\"timeline-item__card-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">April 2001<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Emmanuel Residence Opens<\/span><p>In 1996 the Board of Directors and staff began to investigate the need for housing for people who were living with AIDS\/HIV. Seeing this as an opportunity to expand our mission, Mercy House opened Emmanuel House, now called Emmanuel Residence, in April 2001 and began operations in June. Emmanuel Residence is a beautiful state-of-the art 21-bedroom facility that houses adult men and women living with HIV or AIDS. The facility was built through the cooperation of HomeAid Orange County and John Laing Homes and furnished through the generosity and coordination of Creative Design Consultants.<\/p><\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">The ribbon cutting at the grand opening of Emmanuel Residence<\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">April 2001<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Emmanuel Residence Opens<\/span><p>In 1996 the Board of Directors and staff began to investigate the need for housing for people who were living with AIDS\/HIV. Seeing this as an opportunity to expand our mission, Mercy House opened Emmanuel House, now called Emmanuel Residence, in April 2001 and began operations in June. Emmanuel Residence is a beautiful state-of-the art 21-bedroom facility that houses adult men and women living with HIV or AIDS. The facility was built through the cooperation of HomeAid Orange County and John Laing Homes and furnished through the generosity and coordination of Creative Design Consultants.<\/p><\/div><\/div><\/div><div class=\"jet-timeline-item  elementor-repeater-item-e80c128 jet-timeline-item--image-inside\">\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card\">\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">March 2005<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">San Miguel Residence Opens<\/span><p>A central issue facing the homeless person is lack of affordable housing. Mercy House responded to this need at San Miguel Residence, right across the street from Emmanuel Residence. San Miguel Residence was fully built with the assistance of HomeAid Orange County and Lennar Homes, generously furnished by Creative Design Consultants and Ambrosia Interior Design. San Miguel Residence comprises of two buildings and provides affordable housing with supportive services for up to 25 individuals.<\/p><\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">The ribbon cutting at the grand opening of San Miguel Residence<\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">March 2005<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">San Miguel Residence Opens<\/span><p>A central issue facing the homeless person is lack of affordable housing. San Miguel Residence was fully built with the assistance of HomeAid Orange County and Lennar Homes, generously furnished by Creative Design Consultants and Ambrosia Interior Design. class=\"timeline-item__card-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">March 2016<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Homefront at Camp Anza Opens<\/span><p>In partnership with Wakeland Housing Development Corporation, Mercy House opened Home Front Camp Anza in 2016. Home Front at Camp Anza consists of 30 affordable homes geared toward veterans and their families in Riverside, California. Located on the former site of Camp Anza, a WWII Army training camp, the project also includes the rehabilitation of the historic Officers Club, which serves as the community center for residents.<\/p><\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">Homefront at Camp Anza, a Permanent Housing Community<\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">March 2016<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Homefront at Camp Anza Opens<\/span><p>In partnership with Wakeland Housing Development Corporation, Mercy House opened Home Front Camp Anza in 2016. Home Front at Camp Anza consists of 30 affordable homes geared toward veterans and their families in Riverside, California. Located on the former site of Camp Anza, a WWII Army training camp, the project also includes the rehabilitation of the historic Officers Club, which serves as the community center for residents.<\/p><\/div><\/div><\/div><div class=\"jet-timeline-item  elementor-repeater-item-b0665ec jet-timeline-item--image-inside\">\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card\">\n\t\t<div Opens<\/span><p>La Placita Cinco, in partnership with Community Development Partners, is located in the heart of Santa Ana, CA and was opened in 2023 . Since then, it has been transformed into permanent, affordable housing.<\/p><\/div><\/div><h5 class=\"timeline-item__card-title\">La Placita Cinco, a Permanent Housing Community<\/h5>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"timeline-item__card-arrow\"><\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<div class=\"timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"timeline-item__point-content timeline-item__point-content--icon\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><i aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"fas fa-calendar-alt\"><\/i><\/span><\/div><\/div><div class=\"timeline-item__meta\"><div class=\"timeline-item__meta-content\">August 2023<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">La Placita Cinco Opens<\/span><p>La Placita Cinco, in partnership with Community Development Partners, is located in the heart of Santa Ana, CA and was opened in 2023 . data-widget_type=\"heading.default\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"elementor-widget-container\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<h2 class=\"elementor-heading-title elementor-size-default\">About This Region<\/h2>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"elementor-element elementor-element-7f2f8a4 elementor-widget__width-initial elementor-widget-tablet__width-inherit elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor\" data-id=\"7f2f8a4\" data-element_type=\"widget\" data-e-type=\"widget\" data-widget_type=\"text-editor.default\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"elementor-widget-container\">\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t<p><span style=\"font-weight: 400;\">Founded and headquartered in Santa Ana, Mercy House has been operating homeless services in Orange County since 1990 when our founder, Father Jerome T. Karcher, met a young man experiencing homelessness. Beginning with a transitional residence serving 10 single men, Mercy House now operates a complete system of care in Orange County consisting of outreach, emergency shelters, transitional facilities, and permanent residences. Our vast range of services assist single adults to full families experiencing homelessness in one of the most expensive counties in America. We are very thankful to the Orange County community for supporting our programs and services over the past 35+ years and into the future.<\/span><\/p>\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"elementor-element elementor-element-55c4700 e-flex e-con-boxed e-con e-parent\" class=\"elementor-element elementor-element-e5eb3e6 e-con-full animated-slow e-flex elementor-invisible e-con e-child\" data-id=\"e5eb3e6\" data-element_type=\"container\" data-e-type=\"container\" data-settings=\"{&quot;jet_parallax_layout_list&quot;:[],&quot;animation&quot;:&quot;fadeIn&quot;,&quot;animation_delay&quot;:800}\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"elementor-element elementor-element-9cd6385 Individuals<\/div><\/div><\/div>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"elementor-element elementor-element-4523acd elementor-widget elementor-widget-jet-listing-dynamic-field\" class=\"jet-listing jet-listing-dynamic-field display-inline\"><div class=\"jet-listing-dynamic-field__inline-wrap\"><div class=\"jet-listing-dynamic-field__content\" >Joseph Residence helps to fill the lack of affordable housing options for 15 formerly homeless and low-income individuals. In response to the emerging need for affordable housing facilities, Mercy House transitioned \"Joseph House Transitional Shelter\" into \"Joseph Residence\" in December 2017 to create a 3 unit, 15- bed apartment building. In honor of its historical purpose and the needs of its previously served population, Joseph Residence exclusively provides permanent housing with supportive services to single, formerly homeless and low-income adult men.<\/div><\/div><\/div>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<\/div><div class=\"jet-listing-grid__item class=\"elementor-widget-container\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<span class=\"elementor-heading-title elementor-size-default\">GET TO KNOW US<\/span>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"elementor-element elementor-element-2e96f8e elementor-widget elementor-widget-heading\" data-id=\"2e96f8e\" data-element_type=\"widget\" data-e-type=\"widget\" data-widget_type=\"heading.default\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"elementor-widget-container\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<h2 class=\"elementor-heading-title elementor-size-default\">THE HISTORY OF MERCY HOUSE<\/h2>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"elementor-element elementor-element-74ba23c elementor-widget__width-initial elementor-widget-tablet__width-inherit elementor-widget-mobile__width-inherit elementor-widget elementor-widget-text-editor\" data-id=\"74ba23c\" data-element_type=\"widget\" data-e-type=\"widget\" data-widget_type=\"text-editor.default\">\n\t\t\t\t<div class=\"elementor-widget-container\">\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t<p><span style=\"font-weight: 400;\">In 1988, our founder, Father Jerome T. Karcher met a young man who was living in an abandoned garage. Father Jerome was so moved by the young man\u2019s situation, he made it his mission to end homelessness in his community. This was the beginning of Mercy House.&nbsp;<\/span><\/p>\n<p><span style=\"font-weight: 400;\">Now, over 35 years later, we provide services to over 8,400 people experiencing homelessness annually, throughout seven counties in California and Arizona. What started with a dream to get one person home, has now served over 100,000 people struggling with homelessness.&nbsp;<\/span><\/p>\n<p><span style=\"font-weight: 400;\">Mercy House provides dignified housing and comprehensive supportive services to a wide variety of people experiencing homelessness, adult men and women, families, mothers and their children, veterans, chronically homeless individuals, persons living with HIV\/AIDS, individuals overcoming substance addictions, and those who are both physically and\/or mentally disabled. Every person\u2019s journey back home is as unique as they are. We know that focussing on effective solutions to end homelessness requires a range of programs, resources, partners, and an abundance of heart.<\/span><\/p>\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<div class=\"elementor-element elementor-element-704ecd9 e-con-full e-flex e-con e-child\" data-id=\"704ecd9\" xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-d62cf26 stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-3efb098 is-active\" data-item-id=\"3efb098\">\n\t<div viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-ffca09d xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-e8f9b2e is-active\" data-item-id=\"e8f9b2e\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-97d3917 is-active\" data-item-id=\"97d3917\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-8581713 is-active\" data-item-id=\"8581713\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-5b6d443 is-active\" data-item-id=\"5b6d443\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-2c4069a is-active\" data-item-id=\"2c4069a\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-599bfc1 is-active\" data-item-id=\"599bfc1\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-c57be06 is-active\" data-item-id=\"c57be06\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-a47a535 is-active\" data-item-id=\"a47a535\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-6a01a43 is-active\" data-item-id=\"6a01a43\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-70748d2 is-active\" data-item-id=\"70748d2\">\n\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point\"><div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__point-content\"><span class=\"jet-elements-icon\"><svg xmlns=\"http:\/\/www.w3.org\/2000\/svg\" width=\"3\" height=\"115\" viewBox=\"0 0 3 115\" fill=\"none\"><line x1=\"1.5\" y1=\"0.5\" x2=\"1.5\" y2=\"114.5\" stroke=\"black\" stroke-width=\"2\"><\/line><\/svg><\/span><\/div><\/div><\/div>\n<\/div>\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-list jet-hor-timeline-list--bottom\">\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-158379a is-active\" data-item-id=\"158379a\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">1988<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">MERCY HOUSE IS INCORPERATED<\/span><p>Father Jerome T. Karcher first began reflecting seriously on the plight of the homeless in 1987 while he was Associate Pastor at Saint Norbert\u2019s Church in Orange, California. His first efforts were to organize a small group of friends to assist the homeless. As Associate Pastor at Saint Anne Catholic Church in Santa Ana, he became more aware of the needs of both homeless and immigrant populations. One experience concerning the needs of an immigrant young man who was living on the streets in an abandoned garage particularly moved him to respond to the local situation of homelessness. This decision inspired him to found Mercy House as a not-for-profit charitable corporation.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-dc985aa is-active\" data-item-id=\"dc985aa\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">1989<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House is first introduced to the community<\/span><p>An article in the Diocese of Orange \"Bulletin\" in November 1989 first announced Father Jerome\u2019s work with the homeless. This article caught the attention of Patrick Powers and the Warren P. Powers Foundation who made the first significant contribution that enabled Mercy House to begin services and hire its first employees.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-28a18c1 is-active\" data-item-id=\"28a18c1\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">1990<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House Begins Services<\/span><p>Father Jerome\u2019s first project was to create a transitional residence for employable homeless adult men which opened in 1990 in a rented a house on North Main Street in Santa Ana. It provided shelter for 10 men and was simply called Mercy House. The \u201coriginal\u201d Mercy House eventually became known as Joseph House. It moved to a permanent location and is now a large, 4-unit apartment complex that houses 21 formerly homeless single men including veterans. Joseph House was remodeled through partnership with HomeAid Orange County.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-f82ec04 is-active\" data-item-id=\"f82ec04\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">1990<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">First staff members are hired<\/span><p>It was clear to Father Jerome that God\u2019s Providence was purposeful as Mercy House received substantial contributions to hire two important staff members in 1990 who would lay the foundation for its services to the poor, the marginalized and the homeless. The first was Larry Haynes who is now the Chief Executive Officer for Mercy House and has creatively overseen its expansion of programs and services. The second was Lisa Marcus whose insights and commitment as the Development Director served this growing organization for 15 years.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-b41bfcd is-active\" data-item-id=\"b41bfcd\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">1994<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Regina House Opens<\/span><p>In April 1994, the Mercy House vision expanded further to embrace the needs of homeless mothers and their children by opening Regina House. Seven families may maintain residency there for up to one year there while the mothers are employed, pursuing higher education or enrolled in a job-training program. Regina House was constructed through the generosity and assistance of HomeAid Orange County and Fieldstone and was furnished by Munro\u2019s Furniture. It also provided space for the administrative offices for the growing organization.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-955e4b5 is-active\" data-item-id=\"955e4b5\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">1999<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House Center Opens (Santa Ana)<\/span><p>As the vision of Mercy House grew, it began to consider a continuum of services for the marginalized and homeless. This expanding perspective created the Mercy House Center which opened in 1999 as a walk-in outreach program designed to address the immediate needs of Orange County\u2019s homeless population through crisis intervention, food and shelter referral, clothing vouchers, transportation assistance and hygiene items. First located at First United Methodist Church in Santa Ana then later at St. Joseph Church in Santa Ana, the program operated for 11 years until at the end of 2010, Mercy House had to make a difficult decision to close the Center due to a substantial decrease in operating funding.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-630580e is-active\" data-item-id=\"630580e\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">1999<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Homeless Prevention Program Begins<\/span><p>Further expanding its vision of services, Mercy House pioneered a Homeless Prevention Program which provides at-risk households financial assistance to remain stably housed and thwart homelessness. Later on in 2009, Homeless Prevention was recognized as a national strategy for addressing homelessness.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-dd03a4e is-active\" data-item-id=\"dd03a4e\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2001<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Emmanuel House Opens<\/span><p>In 1996 the Board of Directors and staff began to investigate the need for housing for people who were living with AIDS\/HIV. Seeing this as an opportunity to expand of our mission, Mercy House opened Emmanuel House in April 2001 and began operations in June. Emmanuel House is a beautiful state-of-the art 21-bedroom facility that houses adult men and women living with HIV or AIDS. It is also the current location for the Mercy House administrative offices. The facility was built through the cooperation of HomeAid Orange County and John Laing Homes and furnished through the generosity and coordination of Creative Design Consultants.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-6dc939f is-active\" data-item-id=\"6dc939f\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2005<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">San Miguel Residence Opens<\/span><p>Housing with supportive services became a key concept for Mercy House expansion when it opened San Miguel Residence in March 2005. A central issue facing the homeless person is lack of affordable housing. Most homeless individuals work, but usually at minimum wage or low income jobs that cannot supply the necessary income for housing, utilities, child care, etc. Mercy House responded to this need at San Miguel Residence that was fully built with the assistance of HomeAid Orange County and Lennar Homes and generously furnished by Creative Design Consultants and Ambrosia Interior Design. San Miguel Residence comprises two buildings and provides affordable housing with supportive services for up to 25 individuals. It serves families, men, women and children.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-1032b0c is-active\" data-item-id=\"1032b0c\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2006<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Expansion to the Inland Empire<\/span><p>In 2006 Mercy House became a consultant with the City of Ontario, Calif. in order to address the increasing challenges with homelessness that the city was experiencing. This began the expansion of services into San Bernardino County, with a plan to open four facilities. The first of these facilities was Guadalupe Residence, which provides 15 4-bedroom apartments for low-income and formerly homeless families in need of affordable housing.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-03e6be6 is-active\" data-item-id=\"03e6be6\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2008<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House becomes operator of OC Emergency Shelters<\/span><p>In 2008, Mercy House was awarded the contract with the County of Orange to become the operator of the Armory Emergency Shelter Program as a testament to Mercy House's ability to be a leader in homeless services in Orange County.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-8fa5668 is-active\" data-item-id=\"8fa5668\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2012<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Permanent Supportive Housing expands<\/span><p>Expansion of permanent supportive housing included the completion of Louis Martin Residence for formerly homeless, disabled individuals and Katharine Drexel Residence I, a low-income single family home. Additionally, during this time Mercy House forged a strategic partnership with housing developer, Wakeland Housing Development Corporation to begin a Residential Services program supporting children and families residents in low-income housing.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-6331c7b is-active\" data-item-id=\"6331c7b\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2014<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Home Front Camp Anza for Veteran Families Breaks Ground<\/span><p>In partnership with Wakeland Housing Development Corporation, Mercy House broke ground on Home Front Camp Anza. Once complete, Home Front at Camp Anza will consist of 30 affordable homes geared toward veterans and their families in Riverside, California. Located on the former site of Camp Anza, a WWII Army training camp, the project will also include the rehabilitation of the historic Officers Club, which will serve as the community center for residents.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-d62cf26 is-active\" data-item-id=\"d62cf26\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2015<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House expands to Arizona<\/span><p>In July 2015, Mercy House expanded to Arizona for the first time. Starting with Rapid Rehousing, Mercy house has now expanded to homeless prevention programs including El Rancho and Franmar Manor. We ended our Rapid Rehousing programs in Arizonia in 2017 to focus on the homeless prevention programs<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-3efb098 is-active\" data-item-id=\"3efb098\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2017<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Bridges at Kraemer Place Opens<\/span><p>After years of anticipation, Mercy House was awarded the contract to operate Orange County's first year-round emergency shelter. Opening the shelter was a huge accomplishment and this new facility would be house up to 200 individuals experiencing homelessness. Bridges has an on-site comercial kitchen that serves breakfast, lunch, and dinner, as well as a medical clinic that allows clients to get the much needed medical attention without leaving the shetler.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-12a1e12 is-active\" data-item-id=\"12a1e12\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2018<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">The Orchard opens<\/span><p>Mercy House opened The Orchard, a permanent supportive housing complex in Santa Ana that took 71 chronically homeless individuals off the streets and helped them get into their own home. The Orchard was formerly a dilapidated motel, until Community Development Partners purchased the property to turn it into the beautiful site we now call The Orchard.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-ffca09d is-active\" data-item-id=\"ffca09d\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2018<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">The Cove opens<\/span><p>The Cove is Mercy House's first location in Newport Beach, and is to date, one of our nicest locations. Located on PCH in Newport Beach, The Cove houses 12 formerly homeless veterans and low-income seniors. The Cove offers a number of eminities and connects clients to housing stability through the case management services that Mercy House provides.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-e8f9b2e is-active\" data-item-id=\"e8f9b2e\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2019<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Costa Mesa Bridge Shelter Opens<\/span><p>The Costa Mesa Bridge is a 50 bed emergency shelter in the city of Costa Mesa. Mercy House was selected to operate the shelter and with a number of community partners we help house the clients while they work with case managers who work on getting them into their own home.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-97d3917 is-active\" data-item-id=\"97d3917\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2019<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Oxnard Navigation Center Opens<\/span><p>The Oxnard Navigation Center is Mercy House's first program in Ventura County. The Oxnard Navigation Center is a 110 bed shelter in the city of Oxnard. The shelter helps house clients while Mercy House staff work with the clients to connect them to permanent housing.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-8581713 is-active\" data-item-id=\"8581713\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2020<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">The ARCH Opens<\/span><p>The ARCH, operated by Mercy House, is an emergency shelter located in Ventura, CA that serves 55 single adults.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-5b6d443 is-active\" data-item-id=\"5b6d443\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2020<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Mercy House expands into Los Angeles County<\/span><p>New Hope, operated by Mercy House, is an emergency shelter located in Bellflower, CA that serves 50 single adults.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-2c4069a is-active\" data-item-id=\"2c4069a\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2020<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Buena Park Navigation Center Opens<\/span><p>Buena Park Navigation Center, operated by Mercy House, is an emergency shelter located in Buena Park, CA that serves 149 individuals.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-599bfc1 is-active\" data-item-id=\"599bfc1\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2020<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Brundage Lane Navigation Center Opens<\/span><p>Brundage Lane Navigation Center is Mercy House's first program in Kern County. This navigation center, operated by Mercy House, is located in Bakersfield, CA and provides 150 beds as well as wrap-around services for homeless adults.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-c57be06 is-active\" data-item-id=\"c57be06\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2020<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Huntington Beach Navigation Center Opens<\/span><p>The Huntington Beach Navigation Center serves 174 adults who are experiencing homelessness on the streets of Huntington Beach<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-a47a535 is-active\" data-item-id=\"a47a535\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2021<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Casa Querencia opens<\/span><p>Casa Querencia is a permanent housing program in partnership with CDP. It's a four story building with 57 units.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-6a01a43 is-active\" data-item-id=\"6a01a43\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2021<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Casa Aliento opens<\/span><p>The Casa Aliento Shelter, opened by Mercy House, is a transitional shelter located in Oxnard, CA. It is a motel conversion project funded by Project Homekey, which is an extension of Project Roomkey.<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item elementor-repeater-item-70748d2 is-active\" data-item-id=\"70748d2\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"jet-hor-timeline-item__meta\">2022<br><span class=\"timeline-title\">Hulen Place Bridge Shelter opens<\/span><p>The 24 bed shelter in Riverside, CA, provides safety from the elements and privacy to our clients in the Inland Empire<\/p><\/div>\t<\/div>\n<\/div>\t\t<\/div>\n\t<\/div>\n\t<i class=\"jet-arrow jet-prev-arrow class=\"elementor-image-box-description\">City leaders celebrate the grand opening of first-of-its-kind housing project<\/p><\/div><\/div>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t<\/div><\/div><\/div>\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t\t\t<\/div>\n\t\t","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>History GET TO KNOW US THE HISTORY OF MERCY HOUSE In 1988, our founder, Father Jerome T. Karcher met a young man who was living in an abandoned garage. Father Jerome was so moved by the young man\u2019s situation, he made it his mission to end homelessness in his community.
